## Codex · 2026-07-14T21:22:37.759Z · final_answer

You’re right. I misclassified WhatsApp. It is a core Cortex “root,” explicitly included in the original idea of connecting all major messaging platforms.

Cortex today is a strong working slice of the vision—not the finished vision described in [Naomi’s original notes](/Users/naomiivie/cortex/RAW_TRANSCRIPTION_NOTES.md:101).

## The original three-layer vision

1. Roots: Cortex understands your complete life across messages, email, calendar, notes, files, finances, people, and activity.
2. Brain: It continuously maintains memory, relationships, commitments, priorities, spheres, and a realistic daily plan.
3. Hands: It safely completes work for you, with approval for consequential actions.

## What remains

| Vision area | Current state | What is still missing |
|---|---|---|
| WhatsApp | Backend foundations exist | Enable the product, configure Meta/WABA/phone/webhooks, connect a real account, ingest messages into unified attention/memory, add approval-gated replies, support history through lawful export/local bridge |
| Complete messaging roots | Partial | GroupMe UI/connect flow; live iMessage Mac bridge; additional Instagram accounts; unified threads across every channel |
| Notes and files | Import only | Continuous Apple Notes/files ingestion, folder watching, source updates, editing/actions, handwriting and image OCR |
| Always-on intelligence | Partial | Assistant loop currently needs manual invocation; make ingestion, extraction, prioritization, planning, and reconciliation continuous |
| Reminders | Stored but not delivered | Actual timing engine, Mac/mobile notifications, snooze/dismiss feedback, urgency levels, learned interruption preferences |
| Relationship manager | Data model exists | Complete relationship graph, last-contact tracking, birthdays, promises, follow-ups, preferred channels, proactive relationship care UI |
| Native Cortex presence | Partial shell | Screen awareness, active-app/UI context, local files, Accessibility actions, permission center, audit trail, signed/notarized distribution |
| Voice-first assistant | Partial | Real “Hey Cortex” assets and spoken testing, continuous conversation, interruption/barge-in, proactive speech, integrated voice email workflow |
| Personalization | Partial | Train/evaluate/deploy the 124-example Instagram+email model; ongoing learning from corrections; relationship-specific tone |
| Local-first privacy | Not achieved | Original vision said personal data lived primarily on-device. Current Cortex stores substantial context in Supabase. It still needs a local encrypted store, user-held-key design, selective cloud sync, and clearer privacy controls |
| Action engine | Partial | Email, Calendar, and Instagram have meaningful approval rails; WhatsApp, GroupMe, tasks, reminders, files, apps, browser actions, and general Composio execution need completion |
| Purchases | Not built | Shopping workflow, cart review, Apple Pay, visible total, Face ID/double-click authorization, receipts and dispute-safe audit trail |
| Finance | Not built | Plaid/bank ingestion, transaction understanding, budgets and financial commitments—without autonomous transfers |
| Contacts/tasks/location | Mostly missing | Contacts/Google People, Google Tasks, Apple Reminders, location/commute awareness, travel-time planning |
| Mobile presence | Not built | iPhone app or companion for push notifications, location, camera/OCR, voice, Face ID and Apple Pay |
| Dream and notes memory | Not built as envisioned | Dream capture, one-dream-at-the-right-time resurfacing, interpretation journal, related-note retrieval, OCR of handwritten spiritual notes |
| Bible layer | Not built | Chronological NKJV structure/licensing, historical cross-links, Scripture-grounded Q&A, and decision on whether it lives inside Cortex or as its own connected vertical |
| Developer/business platform | Partial | API exists, but complete docs, metering, organization accounts, admin controls, B2B workspaces and enterprise privacy are not finished |
| Public launch trust | Partial | Google verification, Meta reviews, credential rotation, real Stripe transaction proof, and final authenticated production exercise |

## What is already genuinely built

- Gmail, Google Calendar and one Instagram account
- Unified email/Instagram attention
- Memory, profiles, vector retrieval and automatic spheres
- Commitments, people, reminders and agent-run data models
- Day planning and Sphere Energy
- Sage Mail drafting with review
- Instagram drafting/replies with approval
- Calendar/email action infrastructure
- Onboarding, billing rails and developer API
- Web app plus Mac tray/wake-word foundation
- Production deployment, CI and security guardrails

## Correct five-agent division

The five coding tracks should actually be:

1. WhatsApp and complete messaging roots
2. Continuous assistant, reminders and relationship intelligence
3. Native Mac/mobile presence, local data and voice
4. Full action layer, including purchases and connected-app execution
5. Deep personal knowledge: LoRA, Notes, Dreams, OCR and Bible integration

I should retain release QA, OAuth/compliance, billing proof, deployment verification and integration of those five tracks.


### Claude 3 — Shared macOS local-data bridge

```text
You are the shared local-data bridge engineer for Cortex.

TEAM CONTEXT:
There are 13 total working contexts: 1 master Codex lead, 7 Codex implementation leads, and 5 Claude support leads including you. You have 12 other agents/tasks working simultaneously.

You support:
- Codex 02: iMessage and Instagram
- Codex 03: Files and Apple Notes ingestion

Work from /Users/naomiivie/cortex. Read and obey AGENTS.md. Do not edit their worktrees.

Create or reuse:
- Branch: claude/local-data-bridge
- Worktree: /Users/naomiivie/cortex/.worktrees/claude-local-bridge
- Start from origin/main.

YOUR JOB:
Build the reusable macOS bridge that keeps iMessage, Apple Notes, and user-approved folders synchronized without duplicating watcher logic in every connector.

Own a new isolated directory such as local-bridge/ and its tests. Do not modify the feature agents’ provider files unless the master lead later requests a targeted patch.

The bridge must provide:
1. Explicit source/folder consent and revocation.
2. Full Disk Access detection and clear setup errors.
3. Stable source IDs and local checkpoints.
4. File-system watching with debouncing, content hashing, restart recovery, and backoff.
5. Adapter interfaces for iMessage, Apple Notes, and folders/files.
6. Bounded batches and authenticated upload to Cortex.
7. Local allowlists, file-size/type limits, symlink protections, path normalization, and secret-file exclusions.
8. Idempotent uploads and deletion/update markers.
9. A local audit log without recording private content.
10. Pause/resume/remove-source operations.
```

- Success response: { sourceId, protocolVersion: 1, checkpoint: { id: checkpointId, sequence, cursor, committed: true }, result }.
- Local bridge sends one Authorization: Bearer &lt;device token&gt;; no source token. Server resolves device token, then source by source id + device UUID FK + same user + active capability.

## Codex · 2026-07-15T00:42:13.052Z · commentary

## Codex · 2026-07-15T00:43:25.378Z · final_answer

Best alternatives:

1. **Supabase Cron — recommended.** Cortex already uses Supabase. `pg_cron` can run every five minutes and `pg_net` can POST to the existing authenticated runtime endpoint; secrets can live in Supabase Vault. No new vendor. [Supabase Cron](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/cron), [scheduled HTTP functions](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/schedule-functions)

2. **Upstash QStash — best delivery features.** Calls HTTP endpoints on `*/5 * * * *`, supports signed requests, retries, callbacks, and failure callbacks. It adds another account and credentials. [QStash schedules](https://upstash.com/docs/qstash/features/schedules)

3. **Cloudflare Workers Cron — inexpensive external relay.** A tiny Worker can run every five minutes and call Cortex. The free plan includes 100,000 requests/day, but retries and monitoring require additional implementation. [Cron Triggers](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/cron-triggers/), [Workers pricing](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/pricing/)

4. **Google Cloud Scheduler — strong managed option.** Supports HTTP targets and service-account authentication; three jobs per billing account are free, then $0.10/job/month. It requires Google Cloud billing/configuration. [Cloud Scheduler](https://docs.cloud.google.com/scheduler/docs/overview), [pricing](https://cloud.google.com/scheduler/pricing)

5. **GitHub Actions — emergency fallback only.** It supports five-minute schedules, but GitHub explicitly warns scheduled runs can be delayed or dropped during high load. [GitHub scheduled workflows](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows)

My decision would be **Supabase Cron**. We would remove the Vercel cron declaration, retain `/api/cron/agent-runtime`, invoke it through `pg_cron` + `pg_net`, keep the credential in Vault, and record every run for monitoring. This avoids the Vercel Pro upgrade while preserving the five-minute contract.

## Codex · 2026-07-15T03:03:15.499Z · commentary

The SQL failure is understood precisely: PostgreSQL enforces a `NOT VALID` foreign key on every row touched after the constraint is created. Migration 020 was clearing one bad commitment reference while two other bad references on that same row were still present, so the “cleanup” update itself violated the newer constraint. I’m changing cleanup to quarantine every bad reference first, then clear all bad references on each affected row atomically.

## Codex · 2026-07-15T03:05:15.658Z · commentary

I’m moving that helper behind a normal library module/API boundary and preserving the same tests and behavior.
